## Feng shui (art)

Feng shui (meaning 'wind and water') was the traditional Shou practice of divination.

Powers
A master of feng shui could determine the ideal location for carrying out certain tasks like casting spells, making magical items, and building secure camps and fortifications. Feng shui was a part of Shou life in every way. A wise Shou citizen would always seek advice from the local feng shui master before setting up a home, creating a compound, or even moving the furniture. The master would give him advice on where to put his front entrance, how to arrange his furniture, and how to avoid bad locations when building. By carrying out these actions, bad luck or evil would be prevented.

Some things encourage either good or bad feng shui. For instance:
 Good feng shui: round doors and windows, red colors, a site near water, front gates with elaborate designs to frighten away evil, and windows and doors facing north.
 Bad feng shui: triangular doors and windows, black colors, doors facing the street, and windows and doors facing west.

## Feldran Exeltis

Feldran Exeltis was the scion of the Exeltis noble family of Baldur's Gate that made their fortune making a fanciful Exeltis Ice Wine. Feldran's stint as the head of the family ended in 1372 DR.

Description
Feldran appeared to be a well-groomed youth with perfectly cut and styled hair. He wore the finest silk garb, the height of Waterdhavian fashion. Even his long fingernails were perfectly manicured and polished. Ne never lest home without applying rosewater perfume.

Personality
Feldran had a narcissistic and abrasive personality while being charismatic and persuasive. He considered his foe, Sir Griswald, to be a blustering barbarian. In himself and his actions, Feldran saw no wrongdoings, thinking himself to be a true noble and an honorable knight.

Possessions
Feldran was usually armed with several enchanted items. He carried a potion of speed, the family heirloom – Exeltis Blade, and a powerful +2 magic rapier. He was also known to wear a nymph cloak and a pair of gloves of discipline that he kept in his room of the caste.

He carried a scented white lace handkerchief to protect his delicate senses from the foul-smelling rabble.

History

A couple of decades before 1372 DR, Lord Exeltis, Feldran's father, was the head of the House Exeltis. He was maintaining the family's wine-making business in the family estate – Castle Exeltis on the Blade Cliffs southwest of Daggerford. When Lord's health became frail due to disease or old age, he passed the cherished family heirloom – Exeltis Blade, to Feldran, a mere child at the time. The Lord demanded a promise from his son to defend his mother, Lady Exeltis, with his life.

With years passing, Feldran Exeltis grew up to be a wild and cruel man. One night, the young Lord accompanied his aging mother, Lady Exeltis, on her late-night stroll along the beautiful Blade Cliffs. Feldran, evidently, forgot the promise he gave his dying father and used the Exeltis Blade to cut his mother's life short. And her body was tossed to the bottom of the cliff. The Lordling claimed it was an accident, and he rose to the position of the head of the family with no contest.

The only person who openly blamed the Lordling for Lady Exeltis' death was her loyal chambermaid of thirty-seven years – Olree. The old woman knew the accident was a lie. However, no one cared for her lowly opinion, and Feldran threw her out of the castle – the only home Olree ever knew. The woman spent her golden years fishing at the bottom of the Blade Cliffs and taking care of Lady Exeltis' memorial built where her body fell.

By the, Castle Exeltis was in disrepair, and the family fortune was squandered away on lavish purchases and senseless gambling by Feldran. His managing of the family resources led to an almost complete halt of Exeltis Ice Wine production, making the bottles that were still out there a precious rarity. The financial situation became so dreary that Feldran took several sizable loans from dubious moneylenders from Waterdeep. In the winter of 1371 DR, Feldran met a young enigmatic young woman who had recently arrived in the city of Baldur's Gate. She claimed to be Princess Soliana from the distant land of Fanloc. Feldran decided to seduce the naive woman while his old enemy Sir Griswald fell wildly in love with Soliana.

In reality, Soliana was a naive and romantic young woman, not a princess but the adopted daughter of the Green Hag of Lizard Marsh. Searching for a grand fairytale romance, Soliana created a story for herself and of her distant wealth and nobility. Several nobles quickly found themselves fascinated with the "princess" competing for her hand in marriage. Feldran tricked Griswald into venturing away from the Gate and quickly swept in and convinced the gullible young woman that Sir Griswald abandoned her for the Moonshae Isles. After convincing his unwitting paramour to join him in the Exeltis' family estate, Feldran hired The Agency of Cartographic Investigations', Waterdeep branch to look into the so-called Kingdom of Fanloc from whence Soliana claimed to have arrived from. The Agency was to determine the wealth of the kingdom, and the organization spent several months scouring the Realms in search of the mysterious unknown land.

It was clear that the Lordling wanted nothing but wealth from his new paramour. He thought her to be his possession, a crown jewel in his vault. Nothing more than a pretty face to make fellow nobles of Baldur's Gate jealous of his prize.

Soliana became unknowingly imprisoned in Castle Exeltis. When Sir Griswald discovered his beloved gone, he assembled his family's mercenaries and laid siege on the castle. However, his forces could not cross the cliffside moat. Young Exeltis informed Griswald that Soliana loved him no more and did not wish to see him. The honorable knight refused to believe the outrageous lie and demanded "princess" to tell it to his face. Desperate for help and growing overwhelmed with despair, Griswald sent his page to fetch a hero who was recently gifted holdings in the nearby town of Liam's Hold.

Soliana fell in love with Griswald in the spring of Year of Wild Magic, 1372 DR, but with his perceived abandonment, she gave into young Exeltis' affections. Soliana did not realize she was in captivity as she trusted Feldran because of his wealth, education, and promise to teach her to be truly noble, like in the fairytales her hag mother told her. When Sir Griswald started the siege, Feldran Exeltis announced that a monstrous barbarian horde was outside the castle moat and locked the "princess" in her tower "for her own safety."

Sir Griswald implored the Hero of Daggerford to sneak into the impregnable castle and lower the drawbridge, allowing the Griswald Knights to storm the stronghold. Even though it was unknown if the Hero was successful, some stories claimed that he managed to sneak inside the castle through old sewer caverns underneath the cliffside castle with the help of old Olree. If that were true, Sir Griswald was sure to have had confronted the Exeltis lordling putting an end to his life, and reuniting with his beloved.

## Fetid Gaze

The Fetid Gaze was a coven of green hags that came together in Faerûn during the late 15 century DR.

Base of Operations
The coven took over the Temple of the Restful Lily, a temple of Sune and bathhouse found within the High Forest. The locale was notable for being constructed around a natural spring that was rich with fey energy. The hags formed their lairs within the adjoining tower that once served as the home of the temple's founder.

Activities
The hags of the Fetid Gaze each used powerful illusion magic to hide their true nature, taking on the appearance of stunningly beautiful elven sisters. They used these more alluring personas to lull patrons of the Restful Lily into a false sense of security.

They hags would offer their unwary victims some fantastic boon for a price, entrapping them in a bargain from which they could not escape. The unfortunate soul would then be physically trapped within one of the horrific, magically crafted paintings that adorned the Lily's walls.

Relationships
Each member of the Fetid Gaze knew one of the three secret processes required to create their cursed paintings. While the hags all worked together to lure in victims and accumulate greater power, each of them absolutely despised the other two and worked in secret to replace them with their own daughters.

History
Originally, the hags of the Fetid Gaze were quarreling rivals that dwelled within the same corner of the Feywild, which corresponded with the High Forest on Toril. In the, the hags became aware of a magical spring in the High Forest that was rich with fey magic.

Wanting the fey spring for their own control, the hags manipulated the Sunite priestess Sylvarie Silversong into handing over ownership of the Temple of the Restful Lily. They began using the house of rest to lure in and forge grim bargains with unwitting adventurers. Their treacherous actions continued for at least a decade's time.

Members
 Auntie Greenbones, the eldest hag who believed perseverance was the key to survival.
 Dread Morgan, who believed fear was the most powerful motivator.
 Vile Sazha, who despised the weak and believed only in strength of will.

The hags were served by several minions, including the cambion Saeth, a hell hound named Morty, two gargoyles and a half-dozen scarecrows that were disguised as bathhouse attendees.

## Feston Bargewright

Feston Bargewright was an innkeeper and the founder of the settlement of Bargewright Inn within the Dessarin Valley in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
Once, he was the proprietor of a simple wayside inn standing alone atop a hill beside the ford. However, after numerous attacks from bandits and brigands, Feston elected to fortify the hill. This feat was accomplished with the help of several Waterdhavian merchants whom Feston had encouraged to join him to share the costs. Bargewright Inn was well-established by 1358 DR.

Activities
Feston owned and operated the Bargewright Inn itself. He rebuilt old wooden sections in stonework every winter. The Bargewright Inn sold a chilled green wine from Mintarn; given that it was an acquired taste, Volothamp Geddarm suspected Feston stocked it because he had a taste for it.

He also chaired the Council of Directors that served as the local government.

Relationships
His brother Aldon Bargewright, was a ranger who led the local militia and worked as the stablemaster. By 1370 DR, Aldon had succeeded Feston as chairman of the Council of Directors.

A descendant and namesake, Feston Bargewright, was innkeeper around 1486 DR.

## Feast of the Moon (Vilhon Reach)

The Feast of the Moon, also known as the Festival of Lovers, was a celebration held throughout the Vilhon Reach, including Sespech and Turmish, in the 14 and 15 centuries DR. It took place annually at Highsummer on the first full moon after Midsummer and lasted the whole night.The Vilhon Reach: Dungeon Master's Reference page 34 says "one night after Midsummer" but this seems in error, losing the information of the first full moon of the earlier Polyhedron #101 article. After all, people are surely too hungover from Midsummer Night festivities to do it all again the next night.

Description
14 Century DR
In the 1300s DR, it was a time of drinking, dancing, revelry, and even debauchery. The night saw much feasting, wild singing, pranks, and people chasing one another through the woods under the moonlight. Friends and lovers came together at this time. Nevertheless, many couples were already married, married in the week before, or married on the night, for it was customary for newlyweds to consummate their marriages on this night too. Outsiders were welcome to join in and some travelers made sure to be in Turmish at the time.

A tradition required that lovers find one another in new and unfamiliar locales. For this night, they would go to a previously arranged place, one that neither had visited before, and travel separately along different routes before meeting up for a tryst. Popular meeting places for lovers in Turmish were Evenstar Vale, Faerie Well, Starfall Stream Pool, the top of Bare Bones Hill, and the shores and hills around the Lake of Drifting Stars.

Although the festival was meant to be carefree, the militias of Turmish were generally especially alert to crime, murder, and reckless behavior that might go on under cover of the chaos and the night. Cultists of Malar particularly used this time to kidnap lone lovers to hunt and slay, especially with wild animals. After the Time of Troubles, militia patrols increased to combat them, and the lovers armed themselves too.

On this day, businesses and most government offices were closed.

15 Century DR
In the 1400s DR, it was more a time for Turmishans to relax and enjoy their rewards of the last year and to spend time with friends and family. Turmishan merchants made sure to return home in time.

The Ferry buildings sat in the far south-eastern corner of Thentia, adjacent to the docks and just under the city's wall.

Structure
The Ferry consisted of several docks, a comfortable waiting area, an office, and two small warehouses.

Services
The Ferry operated light wave-runner boats that ran routes between Thentia, Melvaunt, and Hillsfar. They left Thentia regularly at sunrise, midmorning, midday, midafternoon, and sunset, with only midmorning and midafternoon operating in winter.

## Fennor (Icewind Dale)

Fennor was a Reghedmen barbarian lout of Icewind Dale in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He wore leathers and furs.

History
In the late 1490s DR, Fennor, Gurt, and Rond were in Targos loading supplies onto a dog-pulled sled when Fennor spotted the Uthgardt Elk tribe barbarian Holga Kilgore and, from her tribal markings mistook her for a member of the Reghedmen Tribe of the Elk.The Honor Among Thieves novelization appears to mistake the Uthgardt Elk tribe of the Savage Frontier with the Reghedmen Tribe of the Elk of Icewind Dale. This article presumes it is Fennor who is mistaken, as he seems like the sort who would be. Coincidentally, Fennor shares a name with a female member of the Uthgardt Elk tribe, suggesting they were intended to be the same. Rond knew Holga had been banished from her tribe from marrying a "Southlander", so Fennor said she should cover her markings to avoid angering the Tribe of the Elk. Soon, the gang were throwing mud and jeers at her, but Holga ignored them and left.

## Feltin

Feltin was a commoner from an unnamed coastal village in Northwest Faerûn who perished at the hands of a green hag in the late 15 century DR.

History
Feltin was a simple man and a favorite drinking buddy of Del and Grace. At some point in the late 15 century, his hometown became plagued by ghostly voices calling to townsfolk from a nearby small island. This haunted island became the cause of the disappearance of many villages, including Feltin himself, who followed the sinister calls. In the 1490s DR, Del and Grace sought help from the heroes of a northern Targos town – Forge Fitzwilliam, Edgin Darvis, and Holga Kilgore, after the village's first hireling, a spellslinger named Simon Aumar went missing as well.

With a promise of generous pay, Forge convinced Edgin to investigate the haunting, thinking it to be an easy job. When the group reached the island, they too heard voices calling for help, only to find nothing on the island but killer vines of black oily substance. Subsequently, the group discovered that a green hag took residence on the island. They discovered Feltin's body – a skeletal gnawed remnant of a man. He was killed and eaten by the hag or her servant roper. Edgin collected the holy symbol of Chauntea from the corpse to return to Feltin's friends.

Angered at the "adventurers" bringing dire news, Del and Grace refused to pay the promised fee. They did not see the friend's body, and a simple grain-shaped pendant and a tall tale were not proof enough.

## Feywarden

Feywardens were specialty priests devoted to Corellon Larethian, the Creator of the Elves.

Culture
All feywardens were elves or half-elves, with the vast majority being moon elves or gold elves. They were highly regarded among elves. A drow could not become a feywarden.

Some feywardens served as warpriests, who served as guardians of the elvish peoples and took a solemn vow to protect and spread their works and traditions. They sought to emulate Corellon by becoming experts not only in combat and conflict, but magic and creativity. They had a sacred duty to refrain from actions that would despoil the natural world, even in war.

Abilities
Feywardens were completely immune to the paralyzing touch of ghouls and ghasts, and resistant to similar touch attacks from other undead such as lichs. They were also immune to all hold spells and resistant to spider venom and charm effects.

Feywardens could freely channel the divine might of Corellon. In addition to their normal spells, they could cast sixth sense, faerie fire, idea, strength (including a variant that enhanced their dexterity), abjure, minor creation, enchanted weapon, major creation, banishment, prismatic spray, holy word, and sunray once per day. Goblinoids found it particularly difficult to resist spells cast by a feywarden.

The blessings of Corellon granted feywardens the fleetness of wild beasts, the keen senses of the fey, and eldritch connection to the Feywild.

Possessions
Feywardens did not have restrictions on the type of weapon they were permitted to wield, nor the armor they could wear. All were skilled in the use of the longsword and longbow, and some trained to gracefully swap between these weapons in battle.

## Felmer's Keep

Felmer's Keep was a small island located within the Land of the Lions region of South Faerûn.

Location
The island lay in the southwest section of the Lake of Steam northeast from the city of Yeshpek.

Description
Felmer's Keep appeared to be a man-made fortress when viewed from the waters of the Lake of Steam or shoreline, but the "keep" was actually a series of naturally occurring sheer cliffaces from a dormant volcano. The volcano's caldera was a lush garden tended by priests of Chauntea.

Inhabitants
Various seabird species nested within the high cliffs of the island. Verrick and giant lynxs lived on the island as well.

History
Felmer's Keep was named for an adventurer from ages past who fled to the island and perished there. Some believed his ghost still haunted the island. Others believed he was welcomed by the fauna living on the island and became a priest of Chauntea.

## Fenlis

The Fenlis were a tribe of goblins in the Goblin Marches in the 14 century DR.

Activities
The Fenlis competed with the Neidlig for power in the region, attempting to assemble their own dominion of goblins. They were spurred into this grand goal by the proclamations of Neidlig's King Srubaash, who intended to build his own realm of goblins, orcs, ogres, kobolds, and more in the style of Hlundadim.

Base of Operations
The lair of the Fenlis was hidden north of the High Moors, and extremely well defended by traps of unsurpassed technical complexity.

Relationships
The Fenlis were xenophobic elitists, and believed that goblins should subjugate or exterminate all others. They were unusual in the region for only having goblins in their tribe, refusing to hire or integrate orcs and ogres, and despised the Neidlig for trying to build a multi-racial empire. They raided and warred with every non-goblin around them, and particularly hated the Colchar orcs.

References

## Fennel

Fennel was an aromatic plant often used as a spice or herb, found in kitchens from the Sword Coast to Ravens Bluff in the Vast.

Description
Fennel roots and seeds were the most commonly used parts of the plants. An ounce (28 grams) of fennel seeds was valued at 1 single silver piece.

Usages
Culinary
In the Moonsea region, fennel was used in the making of rabbit soup with thyme. That dish was a favorite of a giant magical cat named Gamaliel in the 14 century DR.

Neverwinter's Moonstone Mask offered its guests mushrooms cooked in a sauce of various herbs, including fennel, garlic, and scallions among others.

Calishite spiced wine was flavored with a collection of herbs, spices, and fruits, including fennel.

Magic
The identify spell required fennel in order to create ink for its scribing.

Fennel was among the herbs used in the creation of a homunculus.

In the days of ancient Netheril, fennel could be found among alchemical ingredients used in potion-making.

Medicine
Some believed that the fennel plant possessed medicinal properties, helping with digestion, weight control, muscle tone, reflexes, and vision, and having many other applications.

Fennel-and-hyssop poultices were found in ancient Cursrah. Crushed fennel petals and stems were also used as a sweet-spicy aromatic together with hyssop, bee balm, sour camomile, woodsy sage, and many other herbal ingredients.

Werejackals were notably allergic to fennel.

## Ferostil

Ferostil was a warrior in the Company of the Bright Spear in the mid-14 century DR.

Description
He was burly and younger than Burlane.

Personality
He liked to trade jests while drunk.

He was merry, though his stares were somewhat lustful, per Shandril Shessair. Per Delg, he would have been too much of a jester for Narm Tamaraith.

Possessions
He owned a shield and a sword.

Relationships
He was a part of the Company of the Bright Spear until its dissolution.

History
In Mirtul 6 of, Burlane was already a part of the Company of the Bright Spear. On that month, they stopped at The Rising Moon in Highmoon. While they celebrated their exploits at the inn, the company's thief, Lynxal, was slaid by a local veteran after trying to pickpocket him. When returning to their room later, Ferostil discovered his shield missing; the maid at the inn, Shandril Shessair, announced she had stolen them. Predictably, they hired her as the new thief.

During the next day, while the company decided whether to wait out the afternoon's mist or proceed in it, Ferostil's vote was for proceeding; when they made camp afterwards, he was assigned second watch. Ferostil fought in the battle that came on Mirtul 12, as they came in conflict with a mule train. He voted for pursuing the mule train afterwards. They pursued it to the Gates of Doom in the Oversember Vale in Mirtul 20, where he was slain by a lightning spell, almost certainly lightning bolt, cast by a magic-using sentry.

The dwarven adventurer Delg confirmed his death two months later.

## Fennibal Bale

Fennibal "Thunder" Bale was a famed half-elven hero celebrated in the nation of Aglarond well into the late 14 century DR.

History
Fennibal was a one-man wrecking crew, and he used his strength in the Battle of Ingdal's Arm of the . In the battle against human infantry, Fennibal charged into his enemies and singlehandedly pushed the entire regimen back. The half-elf gave his life, contributing to the eventual win of his side's troops, leading to the half-elven declaration of independence. Stories of Fennibal "Thunder" Bale's heroics continued to inspire Aglarondans as late as the . The set of armor worn by him during the fateful battle, the Mail of Fennibal "Thunder" Bale, eventually left the Unapproachable East and found itself in the great merchant hands in the city of Westgate in the late 14 century DR.

## Fennesh

Fennesh was a captain of the city guard of Westgate in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Fennesh was a stout and extremely muscular dwarf of intimidating physical presence. He wore a set of splint mail in the uniform colors of the city guard. His brown beard was decoratively braided, and a bald spot shone from the back of the dwarf's head.

Personally
He was a brash and impatient man who valued his time and hated small talk and questions.

History
By the, Captain Fennesh grew increasingly frustrated with the overwhelming bureaucratic paperwork he and his city guard were forced to fill. He thought forms and reports took away time that would be better used for arresting criminals.

## Ferrengrove

Ferrengrove was a village of green elves in Semberholme.

Description
Ferrengrove was the sole home to a community of green elves in Semberholme. The village was a tree-dwelling community, reminiscent of similar settlements of the Tangled Vale to the east. Its strategic location near the northern patrols of the Moonshadows and the presence of majestic oak trees made it an ideal choice for these arboreal dwellers. Notably, the village's elevated houses were shrouded amidst the forest canopy, hidden even from the keen eyes of fellow elves below.

Trade
Renowned for their exceptional craftsmanship, the green elves of Ferrengrove gained acclaim for their superior bows and arrows, which they produced with meticulous skill. These coveted weapons found their way to the bustling markets of Myth Drannor and are also traded among the Moonshadows.

## Ferrous dragon

Ferrous dragons were a type of dragon distinguished typically by the color and composition of their scales, that was like that of various ferrous metals, hence the name. Though they were a mix of good, neutral, and evil, the majority had an evil alignment. Despite them being  the same type of dragon, many hated each other. Ferrous dragons did not have a central deity they worshiped, and instead followed the commands and orders of Gruaghlothor.

 Types
Although there were originally 12 ferrous dragon species before the Iron Wars, the following species are the only ones still known to be alive:

Chromium dragons
Chromium dragons, also referred to as chrome dragons, were the most evil of ferrous dragons. They were also the most obsessed with treasure of all ferrous dragons. Chrome dragons held a surprising resemblance to silver dragons, which led many adventurers to their doom. Chromium dragons lived in arctic habitats. They breathed a cloud or cone of frozen crystals and a bolt of solid ice.

Cobalt dragons
Cobalt dragons, shunned by their own kind, were lawful evil ferrous dragons. Known for their desire to dominate and sadistic tendencies, cobalt dragons made strikingly good parents. They found their home in deep dark forests and jungles and breathed a line of pure, pulsing magnetic energy.

Iron dragons
Iron dragons were the most powerful ferrous dragons and desired to make dragons the rightful rulers of the world. Being the only neutral ferrous dragons, they were indifferent about metallic dragons. They mainly cared only about knowing the current events of the world, and didn't care for combat. Found in hills or mountains, they breathed a cone of superheated sparks or a cone of sleep gas.

Nickel dragons
Nickel dragons were the weakest and smallest ferrous dragons, though they had a lot of tenacity and viciousness. Usually tried to overwhelm their opponents with ambushes and its unpredictability. Typically found living in swamps and marshlands, they had a corrosive acid breath weapon.

Tungsten dragons
The only good aligned ferrous dragon, tungsten dragons were dedicated to good and nothing else. They viewed metallic dragons as metal in its purest and wanted to ally with them. Tungsten dragons were the most serious ferrous dragons as well, with a no-nonsense attitude. Tungsten dragons most often lived in arid wastelands and deserts, which gave them a blast of sand as a breath weapon.

Society
A very high majority of ferrous dragons are lawful and follow a hierarchy amongst their species created by Gruaghlothor, where the most powerful dragon species has more importance and superiority than others below them. From highest to lowest: iron, chromium, cobalt, tungsten, and nickel. Ferrous dragons also have families. A few families can come together to join a clan lead by the most power member. All the clans of a ferrous dragon species are then lead by a greatwyrm, who reports directly to Gruaghlothor.

The tungsten and evil dragons all hate each other, but they must follow Gruaghlothor’s wishes and thus refrain from combat amongst themselves. Because of their current situation, survival of the ferrous dragons is the highest priority and fighting other ferrous dragons isn’t tolerated.

The ferrous dragons all view metallic dragons in different ways. The evil dragons see metallics as abominations they must destroy. Neutral ferrous dragons feel indifferent about metallic dragons, and simply accept their existence. Good aligned ferrous dragons see metallic dragons as the purest form of metal. Despite these views, all ferrous dragons revile chromatic dragons. Gem dragons and ferrous dragons are generally good with each other and get along well, especially with amethyst and emerald dragons.

Ecology
Ferrous dragons had more iron concentrations in their blood than other dragons. This gave them an innate power to sense metals, but also made them drawn to them. Some ferrous dragons were even able to subsist completely off of metals.

History
Before Humanoids populated the world and there were only dragons, ferrous dragons were the most powerful and dominant dragon species. A war eventually broke out, still before Humanoids, that would mark ferrous dragons till the end of times. Called the Iron Wars, at least 12 species of ferrous dragons fought for centuries. Two unnamed ferrous dragon species were brought to extinction because of the war. The war ended shortly after the Nickel dragons joining Gruaghlothor’s side. The other 5 ferrous dragons that hadn’t joined their rightful ruler fled after the war ended, their fate unknown. Ferrous dragons oddly stay quiet when asked about them. Postwar, Gruaghlothor put his wishes out that all ferrous dragon must avoid fighting, despite how much they hate each other, should their species continue to exist.
Notable
Gruaghlothor - The ruler of all ferrous dragons and the first ferrous dragon to ever exist.

Appendix
See Also

 Metallic dragon, a group of virtuous dragon subraces.
 Chromatic dragon, a group of inherently evil dragon subraces.
 Gem dragon, a group of indifferent dragon subraces.

Notes
Despite being called Ferrous Dragons, iron dragons are the only dragons based on actual ferrous metals. The rest are based on non-ferrous metals.

## Felsul flower oil

Felsul flower oil was an expensive perfume in the Realms, derived from the felsul tree.

Description
The oil was a fragrant, spicy perfume much sought after by wealthy ladies.

Usages
An ounce of the perfume provided about ten applications. The user benefited from the pleasant scent, as well as an extraordinary property of the oil that made them more persuasive to others for about ten minutes.

Preparation
The oil was derived from the yellow and purple flower petals produced by the felsul tree when (and if) it blossomed in spring. Depending on the harvest that year, a sack of petals could fetch as little as 5 sp or as much as 3 gp. The petals were then crushed and processed into the oil, with the average tree providing enough for up to  each year.

## Felyndiira Shi'narrvha

Felyndiira Shi'narrvha, also known as Mad Money, was a drow matron mother of House Catanzaro, powerful mage, and leader of her own criminal organization in the 14 century DR.

Description
Felyndiira was a stunningly attractive drow woman (other drow would describe her as having cold beauty), with a height of . She dressed quite lavishly in elaborate robes and silks as befitting her wealth and station, and had a strong fondness for fancy hats.

Personality

In her youth, Felyndiira was idealistic, optimistic, wilful, and curious. She had a strong sense of nobility and obligation towards other drow.

As an adult, she utterly lost her idealism and developed a hatred for the drow as a whole. She became highly paranoid, distrusting, and misanthropic, and as a rule assumed that everyone was a selfish deviant and out to get her. She would never sit with her back to a living creature, and usually reacted very hostilely – verbally or physically – to anyone who entered her personal space, killing a large number of suitors and business associates over time.

Abilities
Mad Money was well-educated and trained in reading, writing, etiquette, ancient history, herbalism, religion, and spellcraft.

She had strong magic resistance and infravision as a drow, and in addition to her powerful and versatile mage spells could cast some innate spells including dancing lights, faerie fire, darkness, darkness, know alignment, and detect magic.

Possessions
Felyndiira typically wore bracers of defense AC 2 and a ring of spell turning, carried a staff of the magi and wand of fireballs, and wore her dagger in a wrist sheathe. A less obvious accoutrement was a ring of wishes she had as a navel piercing.

Relationships
Felyndiira was highly distrusting and misanthropic, with a fearsome and lethal reputation that precluded most relationships. Despite this, she had a strong friendship with the illithid operator of the Think Easy, Brikhalna Ipprszhen, though they always seemed to be be loudly arguing when seen together in public. She adopted Gray'shivnarien Taliain into her crime family, and worked alongside low-level gangsters such as Gnormahlenvaey Catoneriehn and Vikhrumn Coll. Her closest companions were actually a group of wolverines that she polymorphed into drow forms, and used as a lethal "Wolverine Security Shield".

History

Felyndiira was the eldest daughter and heir of House Catanzaro, and studied magic in Menzoberranzan. She had an idealistic and grand belief that she could singlehandedly fix the self-sabotaging and destructive aspects of drow society and lead her people into a better age.

As she matured, the arbitrary, cruel, and capricious nature of drow culture constantly ruined her plans and destroyed her sense of nobility and optimism. She came to believe that the drow people were irredeemably foul and a threat to all that was good in the world, and changed her goals to instead eradicate her own people for the good of the Underdark. She seized control of her House, took the name "Mad Money", and used its resources to build her own extensive criminal organization. Her ultimate goal was to antagonize and disrupt other criminal groups so that drow civilization would eventually be wiped away in a great, all-destroying war erupting from the criminal underworld. Mad Money eventually became known on the surface of Faerûn through the investigations of Gerald of Riveyra, Elliot of Kness, and the Forthright Band of Investigators.

## Ferg Drogher

Ferg Drogher was a human peddler of mementos from the Rivington district of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Ferg was a fair-skinned man with dark brown hair and brown eyes. He fore his facial hair in a thin monstrance and a goatee. Ferg wore a simple leather jerkin and was armed with two daggers.

History
In the, the army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in the Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees along the way. Many of the refugees arrived at Baldur's Gate searching for safety behind its walls; however, most were refused entry – those lacking coin or proper paperwork. The Rivington district became a refugee tent city, igniting xenophobia among the locals. Ferg Drogher, a peddler of mementos from Rivington, despised the newcomers and was among those who aggressively advocated against the refugees.

Secretly, Ferg Drogher was a member of the Church of Shar that operated in Baldur's Gate's seedy underbelly. He was stationed in Rivington as the cult's lookout.

## Fedduk

Chief Fedduk was the hill giant leader of the Skullgnasher tribe in southern Amn during the 14 century DR.

Abilities
Fedduk was quite clever for a hill giant, and was about as smart as the typical human. He used this genius to organize and direct his forces reasonably effectively in his protracted war in the Tejarn Hills.

Possessions
The chief had a special suit of giant-sized plate armor that was made by captured dwarf blacksmiths.

History
Fedduk began working with the ogre magi Sothillis and Cyrvisnea in the 1350s DR as part of their plan to conquer southern Amn, and unified three different tribes in the Small Teeth into the federated tribe of the Skullgnashers. Around 1356 DR they suddenly began attacking Hillfort Torbold and menacing the surrounding region, while using the eastern Small Teeth as their secure base of operations. Counterattacks by the Council Army were largely ineffective, as Fedduk and his giants were far more familiar with the hills and mountains and had many hidden caves and hideouts to use.

The situation reversed when Major Tharundar Olehm won the command of Torbold in 1358 DR and set about training and commanding the garrison. With his expertise, the Amnian forces managed to gradually outmatch Fedduk's efforts, and by 1370 DR the giants were pushed back into the Small Teeth. While Torbold had been concerned with the endless skirmishes with the giants, Sothillis' army had been built up and the Sothillisian War erupted, with the Skullgnashers leading the first attack on Hillfort Ishla and then to Esmeltaran.

## Fela Thesk

Fela Thesk was an unfortunate priestess of Selûne from Glister who was accused of heresy.

History

Fela and her sisters Mirela and Iwna tended to the shrine at the then-trade moot of Glister until 1180 DR, when they were accused of participating in the New Moon Heresy. The three sisters did not survive the accusations, and it was only in 1194 DR that a formal inquest into the matter was performed by the House of the Moon in Thentia. The House of the Moon came to the conclusion that the charges were false and that only Selûne could judge them, formally absolving them of any misdeeds.

The accounts of this tragedy eventually found their way to Moonshadow Hall, and were discovered in 1373 DR by High Moonmistress Dhauna Myritar as part of her investigations.

## Fey flower

Fey flower was a variety of rare flowering plant used in alchemy.

Usages
After fey flowers were dried, they could be crushed and turned into ashes through an alchemical process. These ashes of dried fey flowers had a pleasant herbal aroma of honeysuckle, eucalyptus, crocus, and cyclamen.

Ashes of dried fey flowers were the main ingredient in creating hag's bane potion – a magic concoction that could force a pregnant hag to disgorge the child she consumed.

History
In the, several dried fey flower were assembled by the members of the Anti-Hag Support Group to be used against the green hag that called herself Auntie Ethel in the city of Baldur's Gate.

## Feywing

Feywings were a species of flying predatory reptilians that were often confused for dragons and were used by the Zhentarim as cavalry.

Description
Feywings had bulbous elastic reptilian bodies with three heads atop long necks, a pair of strong bat-like wings, two sets of limbs, and a long dragon-like prehensile tail that allowed them stand upright when needed. Scales covered their hides, but the creatures' coloration varied from dark red to blue-black. Most of their round body was taken over by a stomach. Their limbs ended in weak and soft claws. All three heads had horned snouts. Their maws had sharp teeth that were a telltale sign of their carnivorous diet. Horny membranes protecting their eyes offered protection against arrows and thrown daggers.

Behavior
Feywings were not afraid of battle and would fight until the prey was downed. This confidence was a result of their strength and bodies lacking any vulnerable spots.

Combat
Making up for what their claws lacked, feywings' necks were as strong as tree trunks, and their horns and teeth were dangerously sharp. In battle, they flew with staggering speed and landed vicious attacks with the deadly horns and bites three at a time.

Ecology
Feywings were known to inhabit the Five Shires on the world of Mystara, where they were almost hunted to extinction by the Shires' hin inhabitants. Mystaran mages from Glantri sought feywings for their magical experiments, being enchanted, and trained to be used as guardians.

The creatures usually made homes in ruins, dungeons, and caverns, as well as lone mountain valleys. They were mostly solitary or found in couples. They were somewhat intelligent enough to share the love of treasure with dragons. However, the treasure was used to lure creatures for dinner or use as a bargaining chip.

Feywings' main source of food was cattle: easy prey on which to swoop down from the skies, impale on their claws, and carry away. Smaller-sized prey was also an acceptable meal when no livestock was available.

History
In the times that predate Netheril, feywings were found in the lands around the Moonsea, where they were ridden by kobold princes according to Elminster and several other sages of the Realms.

Trained feywings were in the hands of the Zhentarim in Cormyr, keeping watch from the Stonelands and staying a looming danger over Arabel in the mid--to-late 14 century DR. The Zhent feywing riders were not brave enough to attack the city but opportunistically raided caravans and wayfarers.

## Felmorel

Castle Felmorel was a castle in the mid–8 century DR.

Location
The castle stood somewhere to the west of the village of Hammershaws.

Interior
The entrance was dark-paneled on the inside. There were brass lamps.

At least one guestroom was small but had a high ceiling, with old tapestries and a canopied bed, with candles for light set across the room. A room not far from there, used for rituals, had a glass-smooth floor. Nasmaerae's bedchambers were accessible through a secret path leading to the back of her closet.

Activities
The Lord of the castle, Esbre Felmorel, was known to hold feasts with unexpected guests.

Defenses
The gate usually sported three guards in the mid–8 century DR; as of  those guards included Glavyn and Feiryn. During the wee hours of the night, there was only one guard, who was called Bretchimus. Another man-at-arms present was called Errart. At least one chamber had protections built into the stone. By, the Lord employed one or more mages capable of using spells.

History
In spring of the, Elminster Aumar, a Chosen of Mystra, invited himself at the last minute to a feast at Castle Felmorel, over the objections of one of the guards, Glavyn; he used magic to disable the guard's upper limbs just before the man drew his weapons. Before the situation further degenerated, Lady Nasmaerae Felmorel witnessed the exchange and personally invited him to the feast.

The feast Elminster joined, hosted by Lord Esbre and Lady Nasmaerae, had two other guests of note: archmage Thessamel Arunder, and the warrior and explorer Barundryn Harbright. Before they joined, there were already a number of refreshments on the table, including whellusk, half-shell and all, and also soft cheese with spices. The main dishes included sauced venison, a three-serpent pie that was crusty, and a lizard soup with pepper, roasted boar, and spitted fowl. The archmage was cautiously suspicious about Elminster, who found much agreement with the explorer over the feast. While they knew nothing of Dasumia, the host and guests attested to a "Lady of Shadows" ruling nearby lands, with Thessamel being surprisingly quiet about her.

Lady Nasmaerae attempted to cast some spell to subsume Elminster's power or knowledge during the night, and found herself thwarted by Azuth's warding over Elminster. As a warning, Azuth broke her spell-thrall over her husband and warned her that if she attempted that spell again, she'd lose all her spells. Esbre immediately forced her to recast the spell, resulting in the definitive loss of her powers.

In the aftermath of these events, Esbre Felmorel declared Azuth would be worshiped in his home henceforth. Elminster left before dawn, and the mage Thessamel Arunder followed him out.

During the brief period of spell failure in, Lord and Lady Felmorel were still married, and Lady Felmorel was concerned about the loss of spells, though she hadn't regained her own use of magic.

## Feiryn

Feiryn was a guard at Castle Felmorel in the mid–8 century DR.

Personality
He sometimes snickered at bad times.

Description
He was taller than Glavyn.

Relationships
He was a servant of Lord Esbre Felmorel. As a guard, his fellows included Glavyn, Bretchimus, and Errart.

History
One rainy spring afternoon in the, Feiryn was present when his fellow guard Glavyn tried to turn away a magic user answering to the name of Wanlorn, who insisted he was there to join Lord Esbre Felmorel at one of his feasts. Glavyn rebuffed the man repeatedly, until the man used a spell to freeze Glavyn's arms in a cross-armed position, and then Lady Nasmaerae Felmorel ushered him in, much to Glavyn's annoyance. Feiryn snickered a bit at the man's witticisms.

## Feldrin

Feldrin was a Baron of Galadorna in the mid–8 century DR.

Description
The thin, weak-looking man had a customary sly smile and rarely met anyone's gaze with his big brown eyes. He wore fine clothes, but they didn't fit him right, and kept his black hair short. His breath smelled of parsley.

Relationships
His men wore scarlet, and many were hired from abroad. He sought the death of Maethor, and supported Baron Hothal.

History
In the, Baron Feldrin initially supported the bid by Baron Hothal to become king, and make peace with Laothkund by giving them the mines of iron and silver in the forest. But after Baron Tholone's bid overwhelmed Hothal's in support, he backed Baron Tholone to ascend to the throne of Galadorna, in exchange for Hothal being executed on the spot and Maethor's future assassination. In any case, the king and Tholone were both eliminated with a spell by Court Mage Ilgrist right in the middle of pronouncing Tholone king, and Baron Feldrin was caught in the blast, slaying him instantly.

Reputation
He was known as a restless trickster who spent lavishly and often.

## Femter Deldrannus

Femter Deldrannus was a Sharran faithful during the mid–8 century DR.

Description
He was a bald man with purple and black robes.

Possessions
He looted a plethora of magic from Karsus' Keep.

Relationships
Femter was one of Elryn's Dreadspells.

Abilities
As a Dreadspell, he wielded borrowed arcane power.

History
Femter Deldrannus was one of the Dreadspells sent with Elryn to find and slay a Chosen of Mystra near Westgate two tendays into the Chaos of the Art in the . During the first leg of the trip, he had Uldus Blackram lead the way, while holding the noose around the man's throat. When Uldus Blackram was killed by Ilbryn Starym, Femter found this funny.

Once they found the ruin, Karsus' Keep, Femter helped loot the place of magic, and in particular found a ring that cloaked against seekings and hid all dweomers. The team of Dreadspells then sought to bring down the whole ruin on top of two people they spotted, a self-styled god Azuth and the Chosen of Mystra they sought, by using all their looted magic to hurl destructive spells at them. Once their looted magic failed against Azuth, despite bringing down the ruin, they fled.

When Dread Sister Klalaera appeared and herded them back against Azuth, she used some agonizing power on Femter to convince the others before having him stand and walk. They were stopped partway by Umbregard and Starsunder, two mages that appeared suddenly, and he made another attempt to escape, which was cut short by her control again. She then used him to absorb an attack from Starsunder, which left nothing but chunks of him.

## Fetitia Ledora

Fetitia Ledora was a half-elven incantatrix who founded the Sorority of the Silver Fire, alongside Yhelfanna the Masked, in her quest for revenge against the Cult of the Dragon.

History
At some point circa 975 DR, a terrifying dracolich threatened the destruction of Fetitia's home, the city of Everlund. This urged the woman to locate the origin of such an abomination, an adventure that took her all the way to Sembia, far to the southeast, where the Cult of the Dragon hid its temples.

Before she could fully put her plan into action, however, Fetitia found that she was not the only woman the Followers of the Scaly Way had upset; already, they were embroiled in a tense combat against Yhelfanna the Masked, a capable witch of Rashemen. Moving quickly to join her, the two became fast friends and, through their combined might, managed not only to foil a cell of Cultists but also end a pair of dracoliches that the Cult had hurriedly brought forth. Ultimately, the two women even destroyed a copy of the Tome of the Dragon that the Cult had been keeping there.

From there, in the, Fetitia and Yhelfanna decided to maintain their joint efforts. This union would go on to be known as the Sorority of the Silver Fire, a group that, like their founders, dedicated itself to the pursuit and elimination of Sammaster's corruptive Tome and his terrible dracoliches.

Rumors & Legends
Certain rumors spoke of Fetitia's parentage as being none other than Alustriel Silverhand.

## Fey semblance amulet

The fey semblance amulet was a magical amulet from the Feywild created some centuries before the late 15 century DR.

Description
The fey semblance amulet was made using gemstones that, in reality, were kidney stones of some unknown archfey.

Powers
The amulet's wearer had their intelligence, wisdom, and charisma fortified greatly, similarly to the qualities of many fey that resided in the Feywild.

History
The fey semblance amulet had been "passed down through the centuries" until it ended up in the hands of Mayrina of the Anti-Hag Support Group who sought to end the sinister child-abducting plots of the hag who called herself Auntie Ethel in the city of Baldur's Gate in the . The fey semblance amulet could've been passed down as a reward for ending the hag's life.

## Feast Day Cheese Bake

Feast Day Cheese Bake was a comfort food dish made with cheese, vegetables, and pasta, found in the Western Heartlands of Faerûn in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The dish consisted of two recipes, one for the filling and the second one for the sauce. The filling was made using one diced onion, quartered mushrooms, diced red pepper, a generous heaping of greens (spinach, peas, courgette, or anything else in season), and diced and fried bacon or chorizo. Lastly, the recipe called for a generous amount of boiled pasta.

The sauce was made with butter, flour, Cormyte mustard, milk, aged cheddar, and a fistful of breadcrumbs.

The cook was to cook onions in oil until softened, adding peppers and mushrooms in and sautéing them together, later stirring in meat and greens, while a pot of pasta was being boiled. Then, the sauce was made by melting butter in a saucepan and mixing with flour for one minute whilst on high flame. Next, the milk was whisked into the butter as well as grated cheese. When the pasta was cooked, it was strained and mixed into the cheese sauce alongside cooked vegetables and meat. The entire mass was then placed in a square stoneware dish with a floral pattern and baked, topped with breadcrumbs and more cheese. The dish was cooked until the top was golden and bubbling.

Emmeline Hallowleaf suggested that unsalted pasta water could be bused to feed plants in ones garden.

History
In the, the recipe for Feast Day Cheese Bake was known to Emmeline Hallowleaf, the mother of Shadowheart, one of the Absolute Adversaries. She may have had shared the recipe with her daughter following the conclusion of the Absolute crisis in Baldur's Gate.

The festering mummy wrappings were an unpleasant piece of necromancy-imbued cloth bandages found in the Stonelands during the late 15 century DR.

When worn as a belt of sorts, the wrappings were a powerful aid to an undead, pale master necromancers, or some other creature that benefited from negative energy. The wrappings improved their ability to heal with negative energy, to resist purifying light, and violently reacted to those that struck against them with an effect similar to inflict wounds.

The bandage wrappings were once used by an ancient mummy, later found in the treasure pile of the red dragon Daeronnax, the master of Clan Daeronnax. Given the proximity, he may have stolen them from his neighbor, the Netherese mummy Laeron.

## Feast of Lanterns

The Feast of Lanterns was an annual week-long festival held in Shou Lung and Kozakura and celebrated in the city of Neverwinter on the Sword Coast North as of the late 15 century DR. It took place in the month of Tsou of the Kara-Turan calendar during the first new moon of the calendar year.

Description

Each year's celebration was dedicated to different beasts of the calendar, a total of twelve branches, such as tiger, boar, dragon, ox, etc. Each sign held some significance, predicting personality traits of those born on certain years like those born during the Year of the Ox were said to be jovial and stubborn.

In Neverwinter, celebrations of the Feast of Lanterns included fireworks displays (that terrified Neverwintran dogs), gift-giving, festive activities, and competitions for which participants were awarded lunar coins (not a real type of currency in the east, but celebration tokens of cheap metal and seasonal inscriptions) and fed delicious treats of mochi cakes and niangao desserts. Festival goers were offered cultural education on the festival's nature and the awarded coins could be exchanged for trinkets, gifts, and treats. Envoys from the Eastern Realms offered goods to trade and shared their nations' traditions with those interested in learning. Traditional gifts were dispensed in special red envelopes among the younger festival goers, but in Neverwinter, all ages were welcomed.

History

In the late 15 century DR, the festival was brought to Neverwinter by Lord Neverember’s effort to partner with nations across the Far Realms. The festival was shared with the city's many inhabitants, adventurers, visitors, and Kara-Turan expatriates, organized by Taishi Fei, an envoy. Taishi tasked adventurers of the Protector's Enclave with giving out red gift envelopes to fledgling adventurers, offering the reward of lunar coins and commemorative gold leaf-covered wooden year of the ox coins (or coins symbolizing the appropriate animal), crafted to resemble eastern gold billions. Far Eastern pyrobolists organized firework displays, mounting the rockets onto mortars, sometimes with the help of adventurers. Some of the exotic goods brought to the Sword Coast were miniature ox stots and New Year's oxen, fireworks, and fanciful ceremonial Shou greatswords. During the Year of the Dragon, the envoys brought trained hatchlings of jade dragonnels and New Year's dragonnels with different color scales, as well as a new sweet treat – mooncakes. Other years also supplied miniature trained animal mascots of each of the years.

Notable Celebrants
Yojimbo, a traveling swordsman from Kara-Tur, who offered his blade to the adventurers of Protector's Enclave during the celebrations of the Feast of Lanterns in the late 15 century DR.

## Feel light

Feel light was a psionic devotion of the clairsentience or psychometabolism disciplines that allowed a manifester to translate the experience of light through tactile sensations.

Effects
This power made a manifester's entire body act as surrogate eyes, becoming a transceiver for light waves, allowing them to see whatever their eyes would with light for a duration of 10 or more minutes. This had the added benefit of making the manifester more capable of resisting gaze-based abilities or effects, such as the petrifying gaze of a medusa. If their eyes were functioning normally, the manifester also became noticeably better at searching and spotting minute details. This became even more pronounced when used in conjunction with hear light.

This power granted no wider field of vision and did not function within darkness, not even if the manifester had darkvision. Nor could it counter areas of magically induced darkness, as such darkness outright destroyed or blocked light waves.

If a manifestation backfired, the manifester would be blinded and overly sensitive to light for the next one to ten minutes, with exposure to light causing harm.

Displays
When used, this power manifested a material display.

Users
This power was knowable by psionicists, psychic warriors, and psionic wild talents.

Feel light was found to be exhibited as an innate psionic ability by wall-walkers.

Known/Notable Users
 Teliene Coloara, a drow psychic warrior and illithid slayer.

Appendix
See Also
 Feel sound
 Hear light
 Synesthete

## Feel sound

Feel sound was a psionic devotion of the clairsentience or psychometabolism disciplines that made a manifester's body sensitive to sound waves, translating them through their tactile senses.

Effects
This power made a manifester's entire body act as surrogate ears, becoming a transceiver for light waves, allowing them to perceive whatever their ears would normally "hear" even when deaf, for a duration of 10 or more minutes. This had the added benefit of making the manifester more capable of resisting auditory abilities or effects, such as the song of a sirine, as well as better at listening overall if their ears were functioning normally. This enhanced reception was even greater when used in conjunction with see sound.

Feel sound granted no greater range hearing. It did not function within areas of magically induced silence. And if a manifestation backfired, the manifester would become overly sensitive to sounds for the the next one to four minutes, perceiving any sound as a painful garbled mess.

Displays
When used this power manifested a material display.

Users
This power was knowable by psionicists, psychic warriors, and psionic wild talents.

Feel sound was found to be exhibited as an innate psionic ability by rautym and wall-walkers.

## Festival of the Ruby

The Festival of the Ruby, or the Festival of the Rubies, was an annual festival held in the svirfneblin city of Blingdenstone in the Underdark, and  celebrated in the city of Neverwinter on the Sword Coast North as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
The Festival of the Ruby on the surface included hunts for well-hidden and rare raw eggs that were exchanged for deep rubies, and rubies, in turn, could be exchanged for various mechanical dragon eggs; a clockwork replica of Themberchaud, the Wyrmsmith of Gracklstugh; and delicious traditional deep gnome dishes, such as pho, cha lua, and pork buns.

History
In the late 15 century DR, the Festival of the Ruby was brought to Neverwinter with Zinli Gemcutter acting as an envoy from Blingdenstone. She recruited adventurers from the surface to aid the famous Golden Gourmand of Blingdenstone, Saltlix Potstirrer, in gathering ingredients to prepare the ever-popular 3-Gem Omelette, a.k.a. Encrusted Gem Omelette, with three types of rare eggs. The ingredients were to be delivered to the Feasting Halls of Blingdenstone for a great feast.

## Ferdinand (Neverwinter)

Ferdinand was a human instructor and the keeper of the menagerie at the Adventurer's Academy of Neverwinter during the late 14 century DR.

Description
Ferdinand was a middle-aged human man with chestnut hair and a light skin tone. He was notably more relaxed than all of his teaching peers.

History
Ever since Ferdinand was a youth, he knew he wanted to work with animals but did not care for secluded life of a druid or a ranger. As an adult, Ferdinand worked at the Neverwinter Zoo, and eventually, apprenticed under the Academy's Keeper of Exotic Creatures. When the Keeper retired, Ferdinand took over. In the, Ferdinand worked as an Academy instructor specializing in exotic beasts and monsters, teaching the students creature habits up close. Ferdinand's menagerie included a dire spider, an ettercap, a stag beetle, a hook horror, and an umber hulk, among others. Ferdinand was not the only person who cared about his beasts. Yusam, a local druid, visited Ferdinand's menagerie to inspect the creatures and them being well-treated. However, there were never any issues, Ferdinand cared for the beasts as if they were his children, always taken care of and monitored for injuries and sickness. The beasts were also ensorcelled to make them harmless to the students and the menagerie's visitors. His life was taken in the attack on the Academy that eded most of the school's students' and teachers' lives.

## Fey lingerer

A fey lingerer, sometimes known as simply a lingerer, was the undead remnant of a fey or eladrin that refused to die. They were animated by strong emotions, passions, or simply spite that their long lifespan had been cut short.

Description
The physical form of a fey lingerer was zombielike and ruined, but driven by a violent spirit. Their features were skeletal, but in darkness or from a distance, they could still appear delicate and beautiful as they had in life. However, their moldy or slimy clothing and armor could give them away. When injured, their blood was yellow.

Personality
Fey lingerers were necessarily strong-willed in order to reject their deaths, and generally were animated by their passions, obsessions, or strong negative emotions of hatred, malice, or vengeance against those responsible for killing them. Lingerers were twisted and depraved, having lost all the grace and manners of their living selves. They lashed out violently against any who challenged them, especially those who appeared young or full of life.

Combat
Fey lingerers fought ferociously and with little concern for their own preservation. They retained some of the martial or spellcasting abilities they had in life, and lashed out with necrotic magic.

If slain, a fey lingerer's spirit immediately manifested as a flying incorporeal vestige capable of short-distance teleportation. These vestiges continued to attack their enemies in a vengeful frenzy. The vestiges of warriors manifested ghostly swords while the vestiges of spellcasters fired magical rays.

Society
Lingering warriors were often motivated to relive their past glories or to accomplish an unfinished task, and fought with rage and desperation. Lingering spellcasters sought to rediscover lost lore or spells, and were suffused with despair.

Fey lingerers sometimes congregated in groups not far from where living fey or eladrin resided, notably in the Winterglen forest of Sarifal nearby to Citadel Umbra. They were sometimes accompanied by loyal living eladrin, elves, or bralani allies, and other times were encountered alongside their fellow undead.

## Feston Bargewright (descendant)

Goodman Feston Bargewright was the owner and innkeeper of the Old Bargewright inn in the settlement of Bargewright Inn within the Dessarin Valley in the late 15 century DR. He was a descendant and namesake of the settlement's founder.

Activities
As well as the inn, circa 1479 DR, Feston also oversaw access to a secret Harper portal located in a cave beneath the inn that linked to portals in Everlund, a root cellar in Noanar's Hold, and elsewhere. He aided those Harper agents who made use of it; he could be contacted through an exchange of passphrases relating coded information and flashing a Harper symbol, and would grant the key to the room with the secret passage. He would also ask the agents for news. He liked the excitement and intrigue of this duty, but knew too little about the mysterious elderly woman who guarded the portal itself.

History

On Ches 21, the, Feston dealt with Brin when he negotiated for him and Havilar to use the Harper portal to reach Noanar's Hold.

## Feast of the Stags

The Feast of the Stags, also known as the Feast of Stags, was a two-day holy rite observed by followers of Malar from Highharvestide through Marpenoth 1 each year. It marked the highest point in the relationship between most communities and the church of Malar, and was one of the only activities undertaken by Malarites that was appreciated by those outside the faith.

Activities
For a tenday leading up to Highharvestide, the faithful of Malar embarked on a frenzy of hunting and killing of wild beasts in order to collect a vast amount of meat. On Highharvestide itself, they paraded through settled areas bearing the heads of their kills, and invited everyone to join them in feasting on the meats (as well as other side dishes). They explicitly invited those who were not followers of Malar, and honored "the Peace of the Table", meaning that even druids and other rivals of the faith were welcome to join in the gluttony.

During the feasts, Malarite clerics would make public declarations to hunt throughout the winter months in order to provide food for specific individuals in need, generally including the elderly, the infirm, orphans, and widows.

History
The rite was said to have been introduced in ancient times by Garmos Saernclaws.

Following the apparent death of Malar in the Spellplague, different interpretations of the Feast of Stags came to be practiced among factions of the Black Blood tribe on Moray in the Moonshae Isles. As of the 1470s DR, the lycanthropes led by Lady Amaranth at Caer Moray preached peace with their neighbors, and would bring food and smoked meats to any starving human communities as their observance of the Feast of Stags. Meanwhile, the lycanthropes led by Argon Bael saw this as a heresy, arguing that to observe the merciful Feast of Stags without observing other rites (such as blood sacrifices) was a perversion of their faith.

## Feyduster

Feyduster was a variety of enchanted short swords created on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR, with the original being the masterwork of the evil enchanter Nolls's twisted mind.

Powers
There existed three variants of Feydusters, each possessing +1, +2, or +8 enchantments what made the sword's edge deadlier and guided the wielder's hand towards the target. All three versions could affect the target with the sleep spell on a successful attack.

Creation
A Feyduster could be created from a simple enchanted short sword and a vial of pixie dust.

History
The very first and powerful version of a Feyduster was created by Nolls. The evil enchanted used pixie dust harvested from crushed and mutilated pixies to weave the sleep magics into the short sword. However, druids claimed that it was the death screams of tortured pixies that sounded in the sword's targets' heads was what rendered them unconscious and not sleep magic.

More common versions of Fey Dusters with a simpler +1 enchantment were forged by Master Blacksmith Marrok of the Shining Knight Arms and Armor shop in Neverwinter to help the heroes who sought the cure for the dreaded Wailing Death in the . Later the same year, more powerful versions were created by Master Blacksmith Barun of Beorunna's Well to help the forces of Neverwinter stand against Lady Aribeth the Betrayer, who led a marching army of Luskan in the deadly but short-lived Neverwinter-Luskan war.

## Fey spear

Fey spears were a variety of enchanted polearms created on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR that originated from Neverwinter Wood's fey inhabitants.

Powers
There existed three variants of fey spears, with +1, +3, or +8 enchantments that made the spearheads deadlier and guided the wielder's hand towards the target. All three variants dealt lightning damage in addition to piercing the enemy flesh and had a chance of dazing opponents with each successful attack.

Fey spears could be created from an enchanted spear and some fairy dust.

History
The first of the fey spears was a powerful weapon created by a group of dryads and nymphs when the Spirit of the Neverwinter Woods was wounded in a crisis that took place shortly before the . This powerful fey spear was to be used to defend the fey's home in Neverwinter Wood. But before the dryads and nymphs were able to find a hero to protect the forest, the crisis was over, and the fey spear was discarded and forgotten.

Fey spears with a simpler +1 enchantment were forged by Master Blacksmith Marrok of the Shining Knight Arms and Armor shop of Neverwinter to help the heroes who sought the cure for the dreaded Wailing Death of the . Later the same year, more powerful versions were created by Master Blacksmith Barun of Beorunna's Well to help the forces of Neverwinter stand against Lady Aribeth the Betrayer, who led a marching army of Luskan in the deadly but short-lived Neverwinter–Luskan war.

## Feathered ilhuilli

Feathered ilhuilli and its more powerful variant, exalted feathered ilhuilli, were a variety of artifact created to be used by cleric adventurers in the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru in the late 15 century DR.

Description
These feathered ilhuilli were testaments of their owners' strength and the influence of their guilds. These magic items could be created by the most competent weaponscrafters of Chult with the use of soulfired obsidian, hardened bronzewood, fanged Chultan ornaments, and lakh varnish.

Powers
Feathered ilhuilli could be enchanted to house various additional magics for each owner, in addition to boosting the owner's accuracy, battle prowess, and their ability to land well-aimed devastating and lucky strikes. When a feathered ilhuilli was wielded with a feathered teotlanextli, both items boosted eachother's magical powers, increasing the wielder's damage output, healing magics, and reducing incoming damage.

History
Feathered ilhuilli were created in the late 15 century DR and their design suggested the items' origins to be tied to the ancient Lost City of Omu.

Relationships

His lady love was once swept off her feet, seduced into leaving Ferguson, and then driven to suicide within a week by a man whose arms bore a very specific heraldic device.

## Fedhiyajar

The Fedhiyajar, the Granary Towns was an expansive area of farms, villages, and other agricultural infrastructure inside Shoonach, the capital complex of the Shoon Imperium.

Geography
The Fedhiyajar took up the bulk of Shoonach's area within the massive external walls, surrounding the Imperial City, Iltakar, Agis, Mamlukkar, and Debukher.

Description
The Fedhiyajar comprised dozens of villages scattered around Shoonach, attending to the farmlands that fed the city. Following the destruction of Shoonach, it turned to wilderness dotted with ruins, with both wild descendents of crops and monstrous plants growing there.

History
The specific form of the Fedhiyajar was developed from, when Shoonach's construction began. It was devoted to feeding the hundreds of thousands that lived there.

In 450 DR Prince Strohm I slew Emperor Amahl Shoon VII and destroyed the city in the Great Fires of Shoonach. Like the rest of Shoonach it was abandoned, with the farmlands reverting to wilderness.

Inhabitants
By the mid-14 century DR, the Fedhiyajar was home to wild crops such as wheat, and more aggressive plants like tendriculoses.

## Feylights

Feylights was an Evermeet warlock spell that allowed the caster to create spheres of silver flame dancing around them, driving back shadows and revealing hidden objects and creatures.

Effects
This arcane spell created a zone of bright light around the casting warlock. All insubstantial creatures lost their light-creating qualities while in that zone. All effects of concealment (including invisibility) were negated within that zone, similarly to the pre-Spellplague variant of the spell faerie fire.

This zone shrank or expanded, based on the caster's choice and lasted for a short time.

## Felnis

Felnis was a paladin of the Silver Flame in the world of Eberron.

Description
Felnis was a human man with ruddy skin and short brown hair. He wore a suit of scale mail and carried a round shield, shortsword, and quiver of arrows.

History
During the Darkening of Faerûn, Felnis was one of the leading Silver Flame investigators who pursued the Lords of Dust in Stormreach. Following the raid on the cult hideout, their ritual reached completion and blasted a hole into Khyber, allowing access to the road to Tarath Marad and the Spinner's Prison. Felnis knew exactly what the enemy goal was, and sent a team underground to find Brother Aurelius and secure the prison before the Spinner of Shadows was freed.

## Felrenden

Felrenden was a valley on the western end of the the Sword Mountains, where Ieirithymbul was.

Geography
Felrenden was sited between three peaks: Mount Stemhelm to the north, Mount Ardabad to the east, and Mount Pheldaer to the west. The valley had an eponymous stream flowing north-to-south across it, only to vanish into a sinkhole, Braeder's Pit. Ieirithymbul, a gnomish small city, lay on that valley.

There were rich iron and copper lodes, as well as a huge coal seam.

Inhabitants
Of note, Felrenden boasted Palarandusk, a largely unknown entity who was actually a gold dragon.

Rumors & Legends
Palarandusk, the Unseen Protector, was not widely known to be a gold dragon, and there were many who suspected him to be a mage of sorts.
